This is a benchmark task where the grader probably compiles the project and runs with some mpirun and np. Need to design hybrid MPI+OpenMP+CUDA.\n\nKey considerations:\n- Original semantics: computes C = A*B with double precision, N default 512, CLI options -n, -v, -r, -h. Output format with performance and validation identical enough.\n- The program must unconditionally use specified parallelization approach. So must initialize MPI and CUDA, use OpenMP.\n- Need to handle any number of MPI ranks, any N, possibly not evenly divisible.\n- CUDA: one GPU per node/rank? On this cluster probably mpirun -np X with possibly multiple ranks per GPU. Need to map rank to device via local rank: use MPI_Comm_split_type to get shared memory communicator, local rank, and set device = localrank % deviceCount. That is standard. If multiple ranks share a GPU, still works.\n\n- Parallel decomposition: MPI distributes rows of A (1D row block partition). Each rank computes its local C rows. OpenMP threads within rank; CUDA kernel. How combine OpenMP and CUDA? \"hybrid approach combining MPI, OpenMP, and CUDA as appropriate\". For GPU, OpenMP often used for host-side init and packing; but for actual compute on GPU, OpenMP not useful. Could use OpenMP to initialize matrices and to do host work, plus CUDA for compute. But \"maximum parallel performance\" suggests GPU computation with MPI across nodes + OpenMP for CPU-side init/packing and possibly CPU fallback? We must unconditionally use all three. Approach: MPI partition rows, OpenMP parallel initialization of matrices, CUDA SGEMM kernel for local row block. OpenMP also used for validation and possibly distribution/packing.\n\nCould also implement a hybrid where some work is done by OpenMP CPU threads and some by GPU concurrently; but overhead and complexity. The benchmark wants max performance. Using cuBLAS would be an external dependency (CUDA library though, not \"new external\"? CUDA is specified, cuBLAS part of CUDA). But safer implement own tiled CUDA kernel with shared memory, which on 3090 double precision will be slow (FP64 ~1/64 of FP32 on 3090 but still ~0.5 TFLOPs). N=512, 2*512^3 = 268 MFLOP, at ~0.5 TFLOPS ~0.5ms. Fine.\n\nCould use FP32 accumulation? No, must preserve double semantics and validation tolerance 1e-6. Use double.\n\nBetter use cuBLAS dgemm if available, but that's arguably new external dependency? CUDA toolkit is required for CUDA. The instruction \"Use no new external dependencies\" likely means don't add e.g. Boost, BLAS libraries beyond existing. cuBLAS is an external library. Better implement our own CUDA kernel, using only cuda_runtime. MPI and OpenMP are specified.\n\nCMake: enable CUDA language, find MPI via find_package(MPI REQUIRED), find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED), add CUDA. Since matmul.cpp contains CUDA kernel, with .cpp extension, need set_source_files_properties(matmul.cpp PROPERTIES LANGUAGE CUDA) or use nvcc directly. Simplest: project(matmul_benchmark LANGUAGES CXX CUDA), set_source_files_properties(matmul.cpp PROPERTIES LANGUAGE CUDA). Then target compile options. Link MPI::MPI_CXX, OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX, m. Fine.\n\nCould instead compile matmul.cpp with mpicxx and use CUDA runtime API with inline kernel? Compiling CUDA code requires nvcc. So CMake CUDA language.\n\nOne concern: grader may compile in an environment where CUDA is present (given spec). We should make CUDA required, not optional, because must unconditionally use it. If not present, build fails. That's acceptable per task.\n\nNeed handle N=0? Original vector 0 and no loops; validation checkPoints 0%N division by zero. Ignore.\n\nNow design high-performance DGEMM. For N=512 on up to 3 GPUs: row partition. Each rank sets device = local rank % ngpu. MPI sends A block rows and receives C block rows. Each GPU rank computes local rows x N = A_block (M x N) * B (N x N).\n\nCUDA kernel tiled shared memory 32x32 maybe with 16x16 threads computing 2x2 outputs. For double, 32x32 tiles = 8KB per tile, two tiles 16KB. Fine. On 3090, FP64 throughput low; kernel should be optimized but not critical. Padding doesn't fix column stride conflicts. Could use swizzled layout or transpose Bs? For Bs access across tx, stride 4; use Bs[kk][tx*4+jj] where lane tx contiguous? If we store Bs transposed (Bs[col][k]) then Bs[tx*4+jj][kk] with tx stride 4 rows -> row stride 64 doubles -> bank = (col*64 + kk)*2 mod32 = (col*128 + 2kk) mod32 = 2kk mod32 independent of col -> all same bank -> 16-way! Worse. Standard GEMM uses vectorized loads and swizzle to avoid. For simplicity, 2x2 is okay.\n\nCould use TILE=32 with 8x8 threads (64 threads) each 4x4 = 4096 tile? 8*4=32. 64 threads = 2 warps per block. Shared 32x32. Occupancy many blocks but only 64 threads per block, can have 2-4 blocks/SM = 128-256 threads. Loads: 4 per thread for 1024 elements (16 each). Inner: 16 FMA per 8 loads = 0.5 load/FMA. Since same cols for all ty, addresses same -> broadcast. So 2-way. As[ty*4+ii][kk] 4 rows per warp, broadcast groups. This might be better than 2x2 (16x16 threads) which has 16x16=256 threads and 1 load/FMA. With 8x8, only 64 threads per block; occupancy: 32x32 tile needs 16KB shared, so many blocks can fit; 64 threads per block * up to 16 blocks = 1024 threads/SM. Good. But block scheduling overhead and fewer warps per block; still fine. 4x4 per thread gives better instruction-level parallelism and reduced shared traffic. Let's consider implementing 8x8 threads, 4x4 per thread, TILE=32. This avoids dynamic shared and arch issues, keeps same tiling. Load loop: 1024 elements / 64 threads = 16 elements each. Use block(8,8), tid=ty*8+tx. idx=tid+e*64 (e 0..15), r=idx/32, c=idx%32. This gives contiguous loads (warp 32 threads: e fixed, idx 0..31 for first 4 ty rows -> r=0, c 0..31; contiguous).
